# Prior strategic synthesis — passive shared prediction succeeds; incremental law inference remains unproven

The exact diversity configuration is closed. Across compatible latent, centered-loss/readout competition, fresh generic decoders, rank8, learned pooling, convex ridge, fixed-response-bank prediction, post-hoc latent-only adaptation, mixed-context resampling and equal-compute law diversity, no formulation reproducibly beats matched state-only support on independent trajectories across both seeds/roles/splits. At the same time, identical-state active-query experiments and wrong-context controls show that transition responses can contain usable law signal. The scientific conclusion is therefore not “no law information”; it is that the tested amortized/passive objectives do not reliably extract incremental law information beyond trajectory-state support.

The C64 native-variation limitation remains interpretation-only, not a gate rewrite: next-state across-law RMS (`0.588/0.563`) is roughly two orders of magnitude above increment-law RMS (`0.00512/0.00787`), so near-one next-state variation and tiny target-normalized A/B ratios primarily measure shared trajectory state. Centered prediction errors remain the frozen decision metric because state addition cancels in error. Controlled same-query results remain diagnostic and cannot replace passive C64 outcomes. The formerly untouched test split is now opened and is development-only for all later work.

The only supported next passive design is behaviorally trained cross-trajectory adaptation. Two architecture/data/initialization-matched arms are required: ordinary prediction from the encoder code and meta-training through exactly one fixed context-only code step. Both must be evaluated at zero and one step. A single update from A must improve prediction of B increments, and vice versa; target-trajectory responses are outer losses only and never update inputs. Support requires meta one-step to beat both ordinary settings and context ablations, not merely its own before/after result. This differs from compatible-latent agreement (representation matching), mixed contexts (pooled same-input composition), latent-only adaptation (untrained 250-step post-hoc optimization), and decoder heads (utilization after frozen encoding).

The smallest development screen is four fits/4,000 matched outer updates on existing data. Meta adds 256,000 task-level inner code steps and 256,000 differentiated-through-update paths; equal outer updates are not equal compute. State-only, persistence and cyclic-wrong context are evaluation controls, with all outputs explicitly budgeted. A real confirmatory claim would require 16 new laws × A/B/C64 = 48 passive trajectories/3,072 transitions. No numerical work occurred in this synthesis.

Cached code/array inspection verifies that the implementation matches the sealed primary metric exactly. For each condition, `err = predicted_next - target_next`; native per-law MSE averages query/time and coordinate. Centered per-law MSE subtracts the 16-law mean error separately at every aligned query/time and coordinate, then averages query/time and coordinate. Macro MSE averages the 16 laws; strict wins compare per-law centered MSE. - swap/correct ratios improved from `0.9024/0.8061` to `0.8018/0.6854` on native evaluation.

Decoupled/current versus native/current on matched decoupled evaluation was `1.1742/1.2153` in absolute error and `0.99184/0.99513` in centered error. Decoupled+scaled produced strong matched-context centered separation but worse absolute accuracy, weaker A/B consistency and catastrophic native-context transfer. Thus a common context bank changes coverage/observability and can expose law signal, but this experiment does not support it as a reusable encoder improvement or causal proof of a state shortcut.

The scale-conditioned arms were function-matched to current-input arms at initialization (maximum discrepancy `1.11e-16`); AdamW was otherwise unchanged. The supported conclusion is specific: the original encoder input underconditioned the observed-increment channel, and independent train-only increment conditioning improves function-level law prediction reproducibly on viewed laws.

# Current result — overhead and route degradation separated; portable signal question is now explicit

Backtracking/profile loss ratios were `1.0000`, `3.4387`, `3.3770`, and `2.8517`. At equal accepted updates they were `1.0000`, `3.0043`, `3.1389`, and `1.2509`; at equal joint models, `1.0000`, `1.7142`, `2.9584`, and `0.8513`. Thus fractional profiles explain lost update throughput, but not the three equal-update regressions or the two equal-joint-model regressions. The intervention changed recovery paths. Companion_6 supplies no later comparison beyond its inherited numerical stop.

Exact-profiled hidden Powell was matched to the same four immutable starts and baseline profile horizons, not to joint solves or CPU time. It differed from the prior larger-N2 experiment by eliminating the head at every local-recovery evaluation rather than extending the joint 22-parameter initializer search before a separate N3 method. Its loss ratios `3.64e4`, `121.93`, `7.31e6`, and `2.06e5` reject this exact direct-search configuration without closing other solver families.

The surviving explanation is a route-control problem: tangent reachability is informative, nonlinear profiled displacement fidelity is unreliable, local descent alone can choose a worse route, and discarding the joint model is inefficient. Saved histories provide leakage-free candidate supervision—proposal-time geometry and lagged fidelity paired with native one-step fidelity, short-horizon progress, certificates, and work—but not enough independent cases to fit a learned controller.

Result: `CACHED_CROSS_CASE_RECOVERY_SYNTHESIS_COMPLETE`. One zero-solve whole-case leave-one-out analysis can test whether those fixed observables carry portable information beyond predicted coverage. No new recovery algorithm is selected, and no claim of generic network inadequacy follows.

# Prior result — native affine-dot rounding reverses site33's frozen-head improvement

The single authorized retry completed its ceiling: three fixed-point network passes and three residual reductions at center `(H0,A0)`, hybrid `(H1,A0)`, and candidate `(H1,A1)`. Its two reported residual failures were shape-only: the checker rejected `[126,2]` versus `[252]` before flattening. Cached flattening found all 252 binary64 payloads identical at both endpoints. All other feature, prediction, scalar and exact-MSE identities passed directly, so the transition accounting is validated.

For the decisive head-only transition, exact arithmetic over captured binary64 `H1` features and stored heads improves MSE by `9.455443616224053e-32`; native affine-dot rounding adds `5.184872533462386e-27` of worsening and reverses the sign. Target-subtraction and residual-square differentials are exactly zero. Sum and divide rounding contribute only `1.4673751957236082e-34` and `-3.362734823533269e-35`. # Current review recommendation — validate exact-head profiled projected poll; no pilot yet

EHPPP is a concrete model-free-acceptance hidden recovery mechanism. At each center it orients a 13-direction positive basis with the existing hidden gradient and Coleman–Li scale, projects candidates into the original box, exact-profiles every candidate head, and selects only the best strict native improvement. Successful polls retain radius; unsuccessful polls shrink by the existing factor `1/4`. It never forms the current 22-variable joint linear model or the failed fixed-face reduced model.

The evidence motivates avoiding predicted-decrease acceptance logic: capped saved proposals had dominant head-change × feature-change interaction, and the reduced model retained the old model's `3/7` descent-sign accuracy. Recovery also develops large case-specific hidden transfer loss, but that observation is not treated as causal evidence for EHPPP.

Recommend a fixed 56-profile saved-center validation only. Support requires improvement at both accepted controls, rescue of at least one of two rejected controls, improvement at ≥3/4 centers, and complete exact/integrity success. Failing any gate closes the mechanism. Passing does not authorize a pilot; a new companion_14 site-32 method-specific branch would then require separate approval under one N1 event/1,500 Powell evaluations and original `500 accepted / 3,000 profiles / 200 head-inner` caps, with zero joint solves.

Estimated validation: 3–6 minutes and 50–100 MB. Estimated maximum pilot: 45–90 minutes and 2–3 GB. No validation or pilot ran in this review. Proposal: `outputs/sho/autonomous_generic_sho_20261014/reviews/exact_head_profiled_poll_review.md`.

# Prior result — failed substitution omitted the dominant cross term

The seven-state hybrid-forward decomposition reconciles the prior negative cached result:

- exact residual closure passed for all seven proposals;
- at all six capped proposals, center-head hidden-feature nonlinearity contributed only `0.21%–3.21%` of the substituted-loss correction;
- the omitted head-change × hidden-feature-change interaction contributed `96.79%–99.79%`;
- this dominance held for accepted and rejected controls, so it explains why old-Jacobian substitution falsely predicted worsening even when native exact profiling improved the proposal.

Conclusion: the failed substitution is not evidence against the broader mechanism. It evaluated a head fitted at new hidden features inside a Jacobian frozen at old hidden features, where the dominant interaction is absent. It also does not provide a prospective rule, because the exact-profiled head is known only after evaluating the hidden proposal.

No repair was executed. The genuinely strategic decision is now explicit:

The one-shot attempt passed its parent guard and zero-work production-path mock, preauthorized exactly 768 operations, and sealed 54 input files. It then failed the random-basis identity check before charging an operation. The cause is deterministic hash ordering in the new driver, not different basis weights: both seeds reproduce the original sealed initial-basis digest under the Stage 1 insertion-order hash, while the driver compared a sorted-key digest.

Costs are zero forwards, zero rows, zero outputs, zero label openings, zero profiles, zero Cholesky/triangular/SVD/eigendecomposition calls, zero predictions, zero scores, zero teacher calls, zero fits, zero gradients and zero updates. All 768 operations remain `AUTHORIZED`; SQLite integrity is `ok`; C0 and C1 opening flags are false. No C0/C1, representation/inverse, or both-seed gate result exists. The partial terminal artifact SHA-256 is `941e8dfb9fc164b5ece2162aab6977f170d84eb9f1bcb00bc1065b699f657690`; the lock is free.

Privileged frozen-decoder solves showed strong capacity (`0.25992..0.39960` separate-bank ratios) and mostly viable shared-coordinate capacity (`0.40988..0.79464`), but cross-bank transfer failed at `1.69580..4.87506`. A final LOSO affine frame map worsened performance to `1.75336..5.09357`, with only `1..5/16` laws meeting threshold.

These are opened-cohort developmental diagnostics, not fresh validation or a candidate. Total new work was 128 scalar fits, 96 privileged 8D solves, 64 affine fits, four frozen checkpoint loads and 128 forward blocks; zero trajectories, teacher calls, optimizer updates or backwards. The decoder is not the primary capacity bottleneck; the train64 passive-to-coordinate mechanism does not yield a stable shared coordinate on fresh laws.

# Joint passive-identifiability function screen result

`JOINT_PASSIVE_FUNCTION_SCREEN_V1_COMPLETE`, opened development only, failed. All eight response-permuted controls passed, showing learned use of observed transition responses. No predictive cell passed its full ratio-plus-reliability gate. The closest cells were B/C0 (`0.819`, 6/16; `0.919`, 4/16). A/B asymmetry was material and cyclic-wrong specificity remained unreliable.

Frozen-checkpoint decomposition found common-query train MSE `0.247..0.393`, A-to-B `0.412..0.534`, and B-to-A `11.376..12.149`. Development code cosine was high (`0.974/0.983` median), so a simple role-frame rotation is not supported. The result is consistent with objective/source imbalance, incomplete optimization, support asymmetry, or remaining representation insufficiency; it does not uniquely choose among them. Exact optimizer continuation is unavailable because optimizer state was not saved. No fresh validation, teacher call or trajectory acquisition occurred.

# Frozen code magnitude and decoder-sensitivity result

`JOINT_PASSIVE_MAGNITUDE_SENSITIVITY_DIAGNOSTIC_COMPLETE`, zero updates. A/B code magnitudes are similar and radial norm matching does not improve cross-role prediction. Decoder sensitivity does not consistently favor the high-error A support. The apparent directional similarity therefore does not hide a correctable scalar magnitude mismatch under the tested counterfactuals.

The asymmetry instead coincides with a `54.6x` A/B standardized target-energy difference. One A law contributes `98.069%` of aggregate A energy, matching the already closed target-energy lineage. This does not prove weighting is the unique remedy, but it makes the per-source-law normalized objective the direct remaining discriminator. That discriminator is blocked by the existing explicit no-data-dependent-reweighting/no-training decision, not by access or implementation.

# Energy-normalized joint-identifiability result

`ENERGY_NORMALIZED_JOINT_PASSIVE_V1_COMPLETE`, opened development only, failed. Ratios were `0.807..1.320`; reliability was uniformly `8..9/16`. Two response-permutation controls passed; all baseline and cyclic-wrong controls failed. The family used exactly two new fits and 600 new updates/backwards, with zero teacher calls or new trajectories. Cumulative paired-family accounting is four fits and 1,200 updates/backwards.

The train-only relative objective reached `0.095..0.122` per source with near-zero code disagreement, but fixed-checkpoint evaluation shows context collapse: A predictions are essentially zero-code and B gains are small. Feature-support distances do not separate the collapsed development panel from the previously successful/failing panels strongly enough to explain the result. The experiment therefore answers its intended question: source-system energy normalization is not sufficient to make the cubic passive summary infer a reliable unseen update rule.

# Structurally odd response-basis result

`ODD_RESPONSE_BASIS_V1_COMPLETE`, opened development only, failed. Structural oddness improved the intended mechanism control: six of eight response-permuted cells pass, compared with two after normalized unconstrained training. One B/C1 cell passes all controls. Predictive reliability nevertheless remains only `3..8/16`, with ratios `0.894..5.313`; no cell passes its full predictive gate.

The odd encoder is not broadly saturated. The evidence supports a qualified interaction hypothesis: balance and specificity were separately achievable in different mechanisms, but neither alone solved inference. It does not establish that their combination will work or that other causes are excluded.

# Query-conditioned operator result (2026-10-21)

The direct query-conditioned passive-token operator passed all eight development prediction cells but failed the required specificity controls. A targeted odd/no-common variant restored response, zero-context, and wrong-law specificity for A/C0, A/C1, and B/C1, but failed B/C0 badly (`19.3375` aggregate threshold ratio). Therefore neither is a demonstrated solution or a frozen candidate for fresh confirmation. Both used only cached train/development arrays, exactly two fits and 600 updates each, and zero teacher calls/new trajectories.

An anchored-odd follow-up passed all predictive cells and all but the A/C1 baseline control. A fixed zero-fit median of all three families repaired that control but missed A/C1 response-reversal reliability by one law (`9/16`; required 10), despite `5.0834x` aggregate separation. No valid solution or fresh-confirmed candidate exists.

The fixed 1.1 reversed-response training margin repaired response reliability to 10/16 while preserving all predictive cells, but A/C1 baseline reliability stayed at 8/16. A zero-fit median restricted to response-specific families retained the same 8/16 failure. This is a development negative, not a near-pass or permission to weaken the gate.

Fixed train-only cross-role prediction consistency improved A/C1 prediction to `0.0980` and 16/16 threshold wins, but reduced response/baseline strict wins to 9/16 and 7/16. A zero-fit robust median still produced 9/16 and 8/16. Thus aggregate transport improves while per-law specificity remains unreliable; no candidate passes all gates.

Query-relative locality produced a clean tradeoff. Learned content plus locality passed prediction but broadly failed controls. Locality-only restored response specificity but failed A/C1 prediction (`1.2567`) and baseline/cyclic controls. Neither is a candidate; no fresh validation was performed.

The subsequent pairing-odd relational DeepSets screen is inconclusive, not a negative: it exceeded both its 600-second and 1.5-GiB ceilings after computation and has no completion receipt. Its saved result is censored and cannot support scientific interpretation or promotion.

The bounded separable relational screen passed prediction but failed controls. A distinct explicit pairing-odd state-response cross-covariance then passed all predictive, response and cyclic gates plus seven of eight baseline cells. A/C1 baseline was `1.27295x` aggregate but 9/16 strict wins, below the required 10. This is the strongest relational development evidence so far, but no demonstrated solution exists.

Training the cross-covariance model on assignment 0 alone showed real assignment transport on training laws: assignment-1 correct contexts beat zero for 51/64 A and 47/64 B laws, and cyclic contexts for 45/64 and 50/64. Development still failed A/C1 baseline at 9/16. Thus fixed-assignment coverage is not the whole failure, but unseen-law reliability remains unresolved.

The 48/16 system holdout, with fit-48-only normalization, shows genuine unseen-system information: correct context beat zero on 13–15 of 16 systems in every assignment/role cell. Wrong-law discrimination was weaker (7–13/16), so the representation transports useful response information but does not yet infer law identity reliably. Opened-development controls regressed; no solution or candidate exists.

Three fixed training negatives did not solve identity discrimination. Against all 15 wrong held-out contexts, correct context was best for only 0–4 of 16 systems depending on assignment/role, with median rank 3.5–8. The learner transfers a helpful context correction but does not reliably identify the unseen update rule. No demonstrated solution exists.

Full `48x48` context-to-training-function identification also failed to transfer: B1 wrong-law discrimination fell to 4/16, and correct-context median ranks across all wrong contexts worsened to 4.5–9. The learner can be trained to distinguish fit-law functions without acquiring an unseen-law metric. No solution exists.

A fixed closed-form ridge decoder over a fit-only rank-32 cross-covariance basis also failed: zero context had lower aggregate error than correct context in every held-out assignment/role cell, and median correct-context ranks remained 6.5–9.5. Decoder optimization alone therefore does not explain the failure; representation stability/sufficiency remains unresolved, without an impossibility claim.

Using all passive rows directly with fixed four-neighbor interpolation also failed prediction. Coverage geometry is strongly role-dependent: A correct contexts are often farther from query states than wrong contexts, while B interpolation error correlates strongly with nearest-state distance. This supports state-support confounding as a major failure source, but does not show the unseen update rule is unidentifiable from all allowed observations.

A per-context affine response estimate also failed both C-bank prediction and most cross-passive transfer, despite strong sensitivity to correct response pairing. Thus neither tested local interpolation nor affine support normalization resolves the confound. The cached evidence is insufficient to distinguish fundamental observation ambiguity from nonlinear support mismatch; no impossibility claim is warranted.

The authorized matched-initial-condition diagnostic resolves part of that ambiguity. Across eight opened laws, identical starting states produced nonzero law-dependent first-step response dispersion. Correct affine operators transferred across the two initial conditions better than zero response in 13/16 cases, but ranked first among eight laws only 2/16 times. The observations therefore contain update-rule signal independent of initial support, but the tested affine statistic does not reliably identify or transport the rule. This is exploratory opened-system evidence only; no demonstrated solution exists.

A fixed symmetry-canonical nonlinear response signature improved matched-IC retrieval to 6/16 top-one and 13/16 top-three, beating response reversal in all 16 directions. It demonstrates that quotienting translation/rotation recovers more portable rule information than the affine operator, but remains insufficient for reliable identity. Its planned system-holdout extension exceeded the declared signature count (576 actual versus 320 planned) and is censored with no saved scientific result. No solution exists.

A frozen canonical quadratic response operator now passes all eight opened-development prediction cells and all response-permuted, persistence and cyclic-wrong controls. Ratios are `0.406–0.681`, reliability `12–14/16`, and cyclic strict wins `11–13/16`. It infers a per-context nonlinear response relation from the allowed 32 passive transitions in a translation/rotation-canonical frame and predicts query increments without IDs or hidden parameters. This is a complete development pass but not fresh confirmation; no demonstrated solution exists yet.

The first amortized learned-student version of that canonical mechanism is censored. It completed two 300-update fits, froze checkpoints and persisted development predictions, but a terminal accounting implementation error treated the 1-GiB RSS ceiling as an exact zero-valued counter and stopped before saving scientific summaries or a completion receipt. The family used no solver targets or privileged inputs, but its scores cannot be interpreted or reconstructed. No learned-student solution exists.

The accounting validator repair passed 12/12 synthetic cases and performed zero scientific work. Frozen training logs independently show both learned-canonical seeds reduced train-only loss by about 97.5% to similar terminal values, with continued improvement through update 300. This confirms stable training execution only; the learned-student generalization question remains untested because its development result is censored.

A distinct learned canonical query-attention model completed validly. It passed all eight predictive cells, all response-permuted controls, all persistence controls and seven of eight cyclic-wrong controls. Its only failure was A/C0 cyclic specificity (`0.871x`, `8/16` wins). Thus canonical geometry enables strong amortized passive prediction and response use, but reliable unseen-law identity remains incomplete. No demonstrated solution exists.

The matched exactly response-odd variant also passed all predictive cells but worsened identity controls: A/C0 cyclic fell to `0.670x` and `7/16`, with additional A/C1 and B/C1 cyclic misses plus a B/C0 response-permutation miss. Exact response oddness therefore does not solve the learned-law specificity failure. No demonstrated solution exists.

On a leakage-controlled 48/16 system split, direct canonical attention retained strong prediction versus persistence on every excluded-system cell (`0.073–0.314x`) but failed system identity controls on three of four cells. This establishes transferable shared canonical prediction, not reliable unseen-rule inference. No demonstrated solution exists.

The learned-basis/fixed-ridge hybrid completed validly and passed every predictive cell, but not the identity conjunction. It solved context coefficients directly from each 32-transition observation set and recovered all B-role controls on both internal16 and development, yet A-role cyclic identity failed on both splits (internal `8/16` wins in both banks; development cyclic ratios `0.985x` and `0.789x`). This is stronger evidence for context-specific identification than direct attention in one role, but the generic learned basis does not reliably transport system identity across roles. No demonstrated solution exists.

Cached design analysis rejects a simple A-only conditioning explanation. A and B have nearly identical regularized condition numbers and only about five effective learned directions, while seed-to-seed cross-support predictions differ materially and training was still descending when frozen. The problem is best localized to underdetermined, seed-dependent transport beyond each observed trajectory, with A query support exposing it more often. The analytic quadratic basis fits observed rows much more tightly but generalizes worse across support, so neither nominal basis capacity nor row reconstruction alone resolves identity. No demonstrated solution exists.

Direct off-row agreement among the four fit-law context-solved operators did not repair identity: agreement loss fell, all B gates passed, but A cyclic identity remained failed on internal16 and development. A shared fit-only global canonical frame then made both B cells pass while causing severe A prediction and control failures. These results rule out simple context-coordinate mismatch and insufficient same-law function alignment as sufficient explanations under the tested learned basis. The unresolved defect is role/support-dependent operator extrapolation, not a demonstrated solution.

Ordered canonical response secants produced the strongest new learned mechanism: all eight development predictions passed and only A/C0 response/cyclic controls failed. Reversing the complete context direction worsened most cells, confirming real temporal use, but exact response gating degraded specificity rather than fixing it. Same-law alternate passive contexts changed errors by `0.624..2.342x`, and even the deterministic quadratic oracle ranged from `0.140x` persistence to a `1.332x` aggregate failure across contexts. Current evidence therefore supports temporal signal plus severe support dependence, not reliable single-context unseen-rule identification. No demonstrated solution exists.

The approved fixed-budget two-episode test also failed the identity requirement. With 16 A and 16 B transitions encoded as separate streams, all four distinct M0/M1 × C0/C1 development prediction cells passed, but response permutation was better than the correct responses on aggregate in every cell (`0.766..0.920x` control/primary), and only the C1 cyclic controls passed. Internal controls failed similarly. The tested two-context ordered-secant fusion therefore adds predictive support without demonstrating update-rule inference. It is diagnostic evidence only, does not satisfy the original single-trajectory objective, and is not a demonstrated solution.

The single-context response/state factorial shows that responses are genuinely used: correct-state prediction degrades by `15.7–308x` under wrong-law responses and by `2.2–15.8x` when responses are zeroed. The remaining confound is that a whole coherent wrong-law state-response trajectory can still look predictive. A shared absolute quadratic prior was not a remedy and failed by orders of magnitude.

Local response derivatives per unit state arclength provide the strongest current learned evidence. That model passes every opened-development predictive, response, persistence and cyclic gate, but fails cyclic reliability on two internal16 cells at `9/16` wins. Reversal sign quotienting and global derivative cross-covariance worsen robustness, so the useful signal is signed and query-local. The required cross-split reliability conjunction is still unmet; no demonstrated solution exists. The attempted absolute-plus-derivative learner is accounting-censored and has no interpretable score.

The approved derivative coverage factorial could not reach scientific execution under its unchanged selection contract. The exhaustive metadata-only scan of `10,103,779` Sobol candidates found 4 admissible systems rather than the required 32. Its sealed fallback stopped before acquisition: zero teacher calls, trajectories, fits, updates, predictions, label reads, or scores. This is a geometric feasibility result, not evidence for or against training-law coverage and not completion of the inference objective. Result SHA-256: `45a5f871e8ca9e7fe426acaa532892a7850d5f0c58e097e5d2c1aed86e63c7b9`.

The missing generic conjunction is now tested and negative. No structure-informed learner was admitted or executed. The owner rejected system-specific physics for the main objective and placed the next turn under analysis/planning only. The proposed smallest discriminator is a zero-fit evaluation of the frozen panel8 learners across four same-law observation trajectories at the same eight saved states; it remains held and has produced no scientific result.

The frozen-context factorial is now validly recovered from its saved arrays after an administrative validator-call omission. No solve or prediction was repeated; 1,152 summary score blocks were recomputed. Correct predictions beat zero for every fit law/context, yet cyclic correct-context wins were only `29..35/48`. Same-law cross-context prediction dispersion was far larger than nearest different-law centroid separation (median ratio `41.93`), while seed disagreement was concentrated in A0. This strengthens observation-trajectory instability and shared-prior/weak-specificity explanations, but does not separate insufficient excitation from failed extraction and does not eliminate optimization. It is fit48 training-panel evidence only, not fresh confirmation or a replacement gate.

The follow-up cached geometry/excitation diagnostic completed with zero new solve or response prediction. All learned 32x17 context designs were numerically full rank, but only about five directions were ridge-effective and panel leverage was very high. Raw normalized support distance and learned-basis leverage moderately tracked panel error within every context, supporting a real off-support/weak-direction contribution. They did not explain context instability: spectral summaries were similar across contexts, and support distance was negatively associated with same-law cross-context dispersion. The evidence therefore supports a mixed diagnosis—weak learned-basis excitation hurts accuracy, while representation/extraction or optimization differences remain necessary to explain trajectory dependence. It does not establish raw-observation non-identifiability or stable recoverability.

The output-space singular-direction attribution rejects weak-direction dominance as the typical source of instability in this learned basis. Strong energy was larger in `575/576` context-pair blocks and `192/192` seed blocks; median weak energy was about `0.23%` of strong for context dispersion and `4.5%` for seed disagreement. Exact cross terms were retained and become dominant in rare A0 tails, so arithmetic means cannot be interpreted componentwise. Weak output slightly improved full target error in `219/384` blocks and hurt it in `165/384`; no truncation model is supported. The typical trajectory/seed instability is already present in ridge-strong output components, strengthening representation/extraction or optimization mismatch over general weak-direction amplification without proving raw identifiability.

The observed-support versus panel-transport diagnostic sharply localizes this basis's failure. Median normalized observed32 ridge error is `9.07e-7`, median panel error is `0.223`, and every one of 384 panel blocks is worse; the median gap is `2.65e5x`. Unregularized projection on observed rows is essentially exact (`3.25e-19` median), so the tiny replay residual is fixed-ridge shrinkage rather than failure to span the observed responses. Yet observed and panel errors are negatively correlated. This strongly supports an off-support representation/transport failure for the frozen learned basis, not an observed-row fitting failure. It remains in-sample diagnostic evidence and does not establish unique inference or impossibility.

Zero shrinkage does not repair transport. Relative to fixed ridge, its median panel error is `1.15x` worse, median context instability `3.13x` worse and median seed disagreement `41.6x` worse; it worsens 222/384 error blocks, 501/576 context pairs and 189/192 seed blocks. All designs are numerically rank 17, but median conditioning is `4.37e9` and A0 has catastrophic unregularized tails. Fixed ridge is therefore stabilizing. The chain supports high-leverage, ill-conditioned learned-basis extrapolation as the proximate mechanism for this family, not excessive ridge shrinkage, while leaving generic identifiability unresolved.

The matched leverage-regularization intervention reduced median training-panel leverage by `64–73%` across all four contexts but did not improve the unchanged scientific gates. Replay error worsened `1.43x`; internal A cyclic identity stayed `8/16`; development A prediction ratios worsened to `1.050/1.753` and cyclic correct-context ratios to `0.873/0.561`, while B remained passing. Conditioning barely changed. Thus direct reduction of this leverage statistic is not a sufficient causal repair and can worsen the role/support asymmetry. The result does not rule out all geometry or regularization mechanisms. A feature-rescaling explanation remains unresolved because the optional direct scale check was accounting-censored and not repeated.

The frozen-output comparison confirms that the leverage penalty did not stabilize the learned operator. Median same-law cross-context dispersion rose from `9.33860e-6` to `9.70758e-6` (`1.03951x`), and median cross-seed disagreement rose from `2.60965e-7` to `3.76789e-7` (`1.44383x`). Panel macro MSE worsened in A0/B0/A1/B1 by `4.40%`, `0.54%`, `1.44%`, and `5.54%`. Between-law separation increased modestly—nearest median `1.04876x`, all-pair median `1.04256x`—and law-output variation increased in three contexts, so this is not a global collapse; however, the added spread did not improve correctness, and predicted-versus-target pair-distance Spearman fell from `0.54884` to `0.53251`. The slight `2.96%` improvement in within/nearest ratio therefore reflects greater between-law spread rather than useful invariance. Classification: `LEVERAGE_PENALTY_DID_NOT_REDUCE_OUTPUT_INSTABILITY`. This is fit48 training-panel intervention evidence only. The separately censored feature-rescaling values remain unrecoverable from saved evidence without repeating 384 basis evaluations; no retry occurred.

Population-centered output analysis shows that the leverage intervention enlarged law-specific predictions without making them more target-aligned. Law-centroid energy rose `10.34%`, but within-law context noise rose `18.09%`, centered seed disagreement rose `186.68%`, and centroid alignment cosine declined `0.00322`. Median per-law alignment also declined `0.00317`; 40/48 laws remained positively aligned. The tiny `+0.00510` shift in median pairwise-law difference cosine was accompanied by a slightly lower positive-pair fraction and worse error in every context. Bias-energy changes were small and mixed. The intervention therefore generated extra output spread plus disproportionate context/seed noise rather than better law-specific response directions.

Mean/residual gradient conflict is present but the fixed residual-preserving orthogonalization is not a causal repair. It altered 137/300 updates, yet fit48 target alignment and context stability moved by less than one percent and the same A identity gates failed. Typical observed-context replay stayed small, so the intervention did not expose an observation-fit failure; it simply failed to improve off-support whole-law identity. The scalar objective was proven exactly unchanged, while compute was honestly counted as 600 component-gradient evaluations and 300 optimizer steps.

Context noise cannot be removed as a separate output nuisance subspace. Although its effective rank is only `1.40`, its top direction contains `84.11%` of context noise and `81.13%` of the true target-law response signal. By rank 4 the overlap reaches `96.75%`. The remaining failure is therefore conditional extraction within shared physical output modes, not an orthogonal output component that can be projected away.

The fold-pure fixed 104D observation-moment probe is numerically unstable and does not establish transferable mode attribution. Five of six unseen-law folds beat their fold-mean baselines, but the sixth has a `1.114e12` MSE ratio from valid law 31/A0; the same A0/law failure occurs in same-law trajectory testing and pairing-destroyed controls. Median law-level comparisons favor intact joint moments over fold mean (`35/48`) and state-only (`32/48`), yet intact-versus-pairing-destroyed separation is tiny (`-0.00303` median squared-error difference, `29/48` wins) and both exhibit the catastrophic tail. High rank/sign scores therefore reflect ordering, not usable continuous calibration. The result limits this fixed linear moment probe only; it does not show that passive observations lack the relevant information or that nonlinear extraction cannot recover it.

Exact tail attribution shows a finite, context-specific support failure rather than numerical overflow or near-zero moment-feature variance. Law-31/A0's response-3 RMS is `2721.41x` the fold-training response scale; squaring normalized response components creates three coordinates at `1.529e6` training standard deviations, and those three contribute `91.05%` of absolute prediction mass. The maximum fitted beta is only `15.53`, and no variance floor is active. Pairing destruction retains the same marginal second moments and the catastrophe, while state-only A0 is merely tens-scale and the other three law31 contexts remain inside the feature range. The same-law A0 fold independently reproduces the failure. Thus the fixed normalization plus quadratic moments amplify one genuine A0 observation-support outlier; the evidence does not transfer automatically to the main token-level learner.

Temporal localization confirms that the outlier is a few-row raw event rather than a trajectory-wide normalization artifact. Source index 61 alone contributes `94.94%` of response-square energy and `69.05%` of raw increment energy; the top three rows contribute `99.89%` and `96.48%`, respectively. Raw increment and projected response maxima already exceed fold training by `33.43x` and `678.0x` before normalization. The dominant row also has small observed separation (`0.14599`) and elevated relative velocity, but these are descriptive geometry only and carry no force-law interpretation. The fixed moment probe's tail is therefore understood and closed. It neither rescues the probe nor identifies the cause of the main learner's general whole-law failures.

Robust quantiles expose transferable marginal scalar information but do not support stable paired cross-products. Marginal-only unseen-law prediction has law-mean MSE ratio `0.257`, Spearman `0.940` and `44/48` sign agreement. Adding paired cross-product quantiles improves median law error on `34/48` laws but worsens aggregate error to `4.033` because law31/A0 still predicts `56.17`; pairing-destroyed is similarly unstable. Intact-versus-shifted median separation is negligible and reverses in same-law testing. Therefore the diagnostic supports one response-mode signal in robust marginals, not row-pair inference, full-law identification or a deployable solution.

The frozen main learner misses much of that available dominant-mode signal. Its in-sample-by-law mode error ratio is `1.241` compared with the probe's held-out-law `0.257`; sign agreement is `30/48` versus `44/48`. Every law has a positive mean scalar residual under the fold-local direction, while context and seed variation remain nontrivial. This is not causal mediation because mechanisms and law status differ, but it strengthens failed extraction/calibration over absence of all transferable observation information. The dominant scalar accounts for about `54%` of total output error and orthogonal components about `46%`, so both scalar extraction and broader operator transport remain active.

Exact factorial attribution localizes the main scalar residual primarily to systematic calibration, while preserving a substantial context-conditioned law error. Fold-common positive bias contributes `62.40%` of residual energy, law-by-context interaction `19.40%`, and law-centered error `13.12%`; pure context and seed contribute only `2.00%` and `0.12%`. Seed-involving terms total `3.08%`, so random endpoint variation is not the principal explanation. Because the fit48 laws and panel responses participated in training, the all-positive fold bias shows that the ordinary response objective is underfit at these frozen endpoints. It does not by itself determine whether task scaling, parameterization, the fixed context solve or transport causes the underfit. Removing components numerically is attribution only and does not create corrected predictions.

The matched causal intervention rejects simple panel-task underweighting. A training-only initial-gradient rule increased each panel task's weight to `3.955x`, yet dominant-mode law-mean MSE worsened `1.26%`, every fold bias remained positive, and mean bias rose from `1.044` to `1.050`. Seed variation fell substantially and total physical panel error improved `2.28%`, but same-law context dispersion was unchanged and A whole-law identity still failed. Thus the altered weighting can stabilize endpoints somewhat without repairing calibration or law-specific transport. Training scale alone is not the operative explanation for the dominant bias under this learner.

Endpoint gradients do not isolate a single remaining cause. Both task-scale checkpoints retain finite weighted training gradients, and their objective histories had not become flat by update 150. However, seed-0 component norms fell by hundreds-fold from initialization, and the two seeds disagree sharply in task alignment: one is mildly aligned and the other substantially opposed. This supports a mixture of finite-horizon optimization, seed-dependent task conflict and local representation/context-solve limits. It does not authorize more updates or show that training-loss descent would improve whole-law identity.

The fixed two-sided sensitivity check confirms that all four endpoints have locally productive training-loss descent at the one predeclared step. Both ordinary and panel losses decrease in the negative-gradient direction, so the local signal is not merely component exchange. Positive curvature is present but does not reverse the step. This closes derivative probing: local optimizability is established, while any effect on unseen-law identity remains completely untested. The next causal question is optimizer mechanism versus representation, not another gradient or step-size diagnostic.

The fresh optimizer comparison shows that optimization mechanism matters but is not sufficient. Bounded L-BFGS lowers the fit48 objective by `14.1%` and passes every opened-development gate, yet held-out internal A identity remains only `7/16` and `8/16`. The learned solution is therefore optimizer-sensitive, but its law-specific response does not transfer reliably across the held-law A role. Both fits hit their accepted-iteration cap, so this is a bounded negative for reliable identity, not proof that all optimization approaches fail or that L-BFGS converged.

Per-law analysis explains the split: on internal laws L-BFGS improves correct prediction in `52/64` cells, but improves wrong-law predictions in `54/64` and identity margin in only `29/64`. Nearly half of primary-improved cells (`24/52`) lose margin, leaving total correct-law wins unchanged. Development margin improves more often, producing its aggregate pass. The optimizer therefore finds broadly better predictors without reliably preserving the observation-conditioned distinction between the correct and cyclic wrong law on held-out systems. This is mixed—some laws improve both—and does not identify a unique failure cause.

The fixed 40/8 grouped-law selection rule is not supported. It selected the fourth of five L-BFGS segments in both seeds, proving that its pooled selection ratio was non-monotone along each fresh path. Relative to the same paths' final checkpoints, it improved selected A/C1 identity but lost B-role wins, leaving internal cyclic wins `41/64` versus `42/64`, with A/C0 still only `7/16`. Development A/C1 prediction also remained above threshold (`1.12073x`). Reduced 40-law exposure and opened-development status remain explicit. The result supports path dependence but not a transferable checkpoint rule or solution.

The subsequent retrospective all-candidate path audit has no valid scientific result. Its exact counter stopped completion after revealing `1,024` context solves against `512` declared, because the count fixture omitted the response-permuted solve. No saved candidate score is interpreted. This incident neither strengthens nor weakens path capacity; it closes this retrospective branch without retry.

The robust response-marginal branch produces a real prediction benefit but not reliable law identity. Relative to the capacity-matched state-only branch, internal MSE improved in all four cells and cyclic wins rose `38→41/64`; relative to saved baseline, all four internal MSEs improved and wins also rose `38→41/64`. Nevertheless B/C0 remained below the unchanged identity gate at `9/16`. Development prediction passed all cells, including the baseline's missed A/C1 cell, but both A cyclic controls failed and total wins fell below both controls (`41` full versus `45` state-only and `43` baseline). Pairing-permutation controls remained strong, so the mechanism is not a simple marginal-only replacement, but its response marginals cannot supply consistent whole-law specificity.

Fixed global rank pairing does not repair that specificity gap. The intact-rank arm loses to the identically sized broken-pairing control on all eight primary prediction aggregates and on total cyclic wins in both splits. It also fails internal A/C1 response use. Therefore no benefit can be attributed to the intact 40 Spearman correlations; the modest behavior of either enlarged model is capacity/marginal or optimization evidence, not robust pairing evidence.

# Current result — directional penalty negative; consolidated stop

The authorized recovery completed the same sealed comparison with no recalibration and exactly one repeated lost 48-instance solve batch. Four fits/600 updates completed; cumulative solve usage is correct `116272`, response-permuted `256`, cyclic `0`, total `116528`. Resources were 20.30 seconds, 370,065,408 peak RSS bytes and 6,372,964 artifact bytes.

Directional and norm-only arms both fail the unchanged internal/development identity conjunctions. Directional cyclic wins are internal `10/10/9/11` and development `8/10/11/12`; norm-only gives `8/10/10/11` and `7/10/12/13`. Both fail A-role development identity, and directional median MSE/orthogonal error are worse than norm-only. This is a negative matched result, not proof that every directional objective fails; the penalty acts on the full predicted increment rather than isolated between-law differences.

No learned student has demonstrated reliable unseen-law inference or fresh confirmation. Reproduced evidence supports a strong transferable shared-dynamics prior, response sensitivity and partial law signal, alongside persistent context-dependent whole-law specificity failure. Scientific execution is stopped. The single recommendation is a decision about retiring this repeatedly tuned learner lineage as a confirmation candidate and, if research continues, beginning a clean preregistered structure-free phase with a new selection path and reserved untouched cohort. Nothing is launched by that recommendation.

# Current result — persistent prior learns signal but not pairing-specific identity

The first compact probabilistic system-identification pilot completed validly. A shared frozen function decoder plus an amortized 8D diagonal posterior learned from passive32 contexts consistently beat empty/state-only priors on encoder-heldout represented laws, separated cyclic wrong-law contexts, and obtained better proper energy score than a degenerate posterior mean. The response-aware fits also ended with lower ordinary training MSE than capacity-matched state-only fits in both seeds.

The mechanism nevertheless failed its central identity requirement. Intact state-response pairing never beat the fixed reversed-response control at the required margin in any of the 12 internal/development cells, and energy identity failed in all 12. Opened decoder-unseen development also passed aligned-versus-empty in only `4/8` cells and aligned-versus-state-only in `2/8`. A zero-model-call attribution found median pairing/state-removal predictive shifts of only `0.0592` internally and `0.0444` on development, with the pairing shift smaller in `174/192` blocks. Thus this fixed encoder relies mainly on response marginals/shared law signal rather than learning the state-conditioned response relation needed for robust unseen-rule inference.

This establishes neither passive non-identifiability nor failure of probabilistic task inference in general. It does establish that the exact frozen-decoder, mean-pooled diagonal-posterior route is not a solution and should not be expanded by hyperparameter sweep. It is reusable offline learning only; sequential persistent accumulation was not tested. No fresh confirmation or calibrated-uncertainty claim is available.

# Current result — self-attention plus masked supervision extracts pairing internally, not across decoder-unseen laws

The causal factorial did not produce a supported solution: DeepSets ordinary/masked passed `0/12` cells, Set Transformer ordinary `1/12`, and Set Transformer masked `4/12`. The important partial result is structured rather than a generic capacity gain: models were parameter-matched (70,288 versus 69,200), while pairing passes rose only for token-token self-attention and most strongly with masked-response reconstruction. In the masked Transformer, internal pairing-destroyed/correct MSE ratios had median `1.95`; three of four internal cells fully passed. Development pairing ratios stayed near one and only one of eight development cells fully passed.

Therefore the ordinary objective was under-incentivized and relational context modeling helps represented-law pairing, but the fixed compact-code/frozen-decoder route still fails reliable decoder-unseen transfer. This narrows the next question to whether direct query-conditioned functional prediction can transport the extracted relation without forcing it through the established 8D decoder manifold. It does not show that arbitrary physical mechanisms are learned; all data remain one discrete two-body force-law family.

# Current result — fixed absolute chronology is not a reliable repair

The evidence-preserving recovery completed the exact sealed two-fit chronology comparison. The original attempt remains interrupted and is conservatively charged in full; the new result and cumulative accounting are bound by `diagnostics/persistent_prior_chronology_v1_recovery1/recovery_completion.json`.

Fixed source-index sinusoidal features produce only `2/12` official passes, `2/12` pairing-clause passes, one pairing improvement over the saved no-position baseline, and two improvements over the same-checkpoint position scramble. Seed-2/internal/A improves, while most opened-development ratios stay near one and fail identity or energy clauses. Classification is `FIXED_CHRONOLOGY_NOT_FULLY_SUPPORTED`. Absolute position may encode shared schedule/phase information, so even isolated gains are not general chronology evidence. No fresh systems were opened and no teacher/data work occurred.

# Result — affine empirical-Bayes prior reduces observation need on opened full-K development laws

Recovered result: `REPLICATED_DEVELOPMENT_SUPPORT_LIMITED_AFFINE_PRIOR`. Validation macro MSE at one transition was `0.222389/0.272887/0.244697` for N48/N96/N192 priors versus `0.540895` no-prior; opened-test values were `0.227462/0.333348/0.267876` versus `0.529992`. Empirical Bayes won on 60--62 of 64 laws in every one-transition prior-size cell. At two transitions, N48/N96/N192 macro MSE was `3.30844e-29/5.26383e-30/2.56608e-30` on validation and `1.93825e-29/3.12346e-30/1.20930e-30` on opened test, while no-prior remained `0.545357/0.649574`.

Correct-context identity passed the sealed rule for all six replicated N/k points, but the one-transition macro advantage versus wrong context was sometimes modest on validation (`0.8062`, `0.9927`, `0.9535` correct/wrong ratios for N48/N96/N192). From four transitions onward no-prior OLS was already numerically exact, so tiny `1e-29` versus `1e-30` differences have no practical significance. Cumulative scientific work was 7,872 coefficient solves, 757,449 query rows and 1,514,898 output scalars. Recovery scientific replay was exactly zero.
