# 2026-10-27 — cross-mechanism learned feature adaptation and confirmation

The materially new controlled-reset benchmark trained a `2→32→16` shared feature map on 72 laws spanning linear-damped, Duffing and Van der Pol mechanisms. A new system supplied 32 controlled state/response pairs; only a `16×2` ridge head was inferred. Family labels, equations and parameters were never learner inputs. On 16 wholly held-out pendulum laws, both learned seeds beat seed-matched frozen-random features, per-system affine fitting and a context-blind network on all `16/16` laws. Learned/random macro ratios were `0.1544/0.1053`, learned/affine `0.1607/0.1050`, and learned/blind `0.00695/0.00475`; cyclic wrong-system context was `110.5×/169.2×` worse. Classification: `SUPPORTED_DEVELOPMENT_ONLY`.

A zero-fit shifted-bank test repeated the result on exact-byte-disjoint controlled states: learned/random `0.1974/0.1689`, learned/affine `0.2004/0.1644`, learned/blind `0.00866/0.00742`, all `16/16` wins, cyclic penalties `88.9×/108.7×`. This weakens fixed-grid memorization as the explanation.

The prospectively reserved saturating-spring family then provided genuine confirmation with new laws and a third disjoint state bank. Both frozen seeds passed every unchanged gate: learned/random `0.1473/0.1197`, learned/affine `0.1482/0.1189`, learned/blind `0.00734/0.00612`, all `16/16` wins, cyclic penalties `43.2×/53.8×`. Classification: `FRESH_CONFIRMATION_SUPPORTED_WITHIN_DECLARED_BENCHMARK`. The first confirmation attempt stopped on a variable-name defect before any completed step; its sealed systems/states and 32 spent RHS rows were preserved. Recovery used bitwise-identical systems/states and counted 1,536 completed steps plus 6,144 new RHS rows.

This is compact cross-mechanism function adaptation, not unrestricted dynamics discovery. The benchmark supplies a common `(q,v)` state, RK4/dt convention, controlled resets and related second-order mechanisms. Result SHA-256 values are pilot `1d81ace77f99982520e88f09283c6cdf3775a986a02864c870df230b43fbcf4a`, shifted bank `99b02dde0aaf8a0caa21f670a0c8103355dc5d04160fe67e7caae2ed8371eac9`, and confirmation `fc2065ca2977efb5df92c8efb7e042fbc9a22386c8b6c6591d9bafa2f159794e`.

# 2026-10-27 — chronological continual accumulation with replay

One fixed family order tested actual persistent shared-weight updates rather than offline pretraining alone. Linear-damped, Duffing and Van der Pol arrived successively. Replay batches retained four current and four prior-system tasks; naive sequential training saw only the current family; scratch-current and compute-matched joint-offline controls used the same architecture and original initialization.

Both seeds passed. Prior experience reduced Duffing error to `0.199/0.158` of scratch and Van der Pol error to `0.190/0.514`, winning every current-family law. After the final family, replay reduced earlier-family error to `0.0166/0.0469` of naive sequential learning, winning `16/16`. Final replay/joint-offline ratios were `0.715/0.980`, with `24/24` and `17/24` replay wins. Classification: `SUPPORTED_DEVELOPMENT_ONLY`.

Exact work was 9,216 repeated one-step simulations/36,864 RHS rows, 10 fits, 5,874 updates, 46,992 training ridge factorizations, 3,007,488 training prediction rows, 368 evaluation solves and 368 score blocks. This supports persistent accumulation under bounded rehearsal in the declared benchmark; it is not memory-free continual learning or fresh confirmation of the sequential variant. Result SHA-256 is `234533ed44073cd9627f0e7defbf4a16509ca243d950d45a9175fefd4eef137d`.

# 2026-10-27 — fresh-family sequential transfer is not replicated; retention is

The first Rayleigh-family execution is scientifically invalid: prior-family replay targets at the original query bank were accidentally paired with features from the new Rayleigh query bank. Preserve its full work and `result.json`, but supersede every scientific ratio from that attempt.

One separately sealed recovery changed only that query-bank selection and repeated all work cumulatively. The systems and state banks were bitwise identical, but the cohort was already opened by attempt 1, so recovery is corrected development evidence rather than pristine confirmation. Zero-gradient replay context fitting was worse than the seed-matched random representation in both seeds (`1.300x`, `1.242x`; `0/8`, `1/8` wins), although slightly better than the frozen offline joint model (`0.941x`, `0.888x`). After 267 matched updates, replay initialization beat scratch strongly in seed 1 (`0.557x`, `8/8`) but failed in seed 2 (`1.320x`, `2/8`); forward adaptation therefore did not replicate. Rehearsal retention was robust: replay/no-replay error ratios were `0.0554` and `0.0291`, with `24/24` earlier-law wins in both seeds. Classification remains `NOT_SUPPORTED` for the full conjunction.

Each attempt used 3,072 new steps/12,288 RHS evaluations, six fits/1,602 updates, 12,816 training ridge factorizations, 820,224 training prediction rows and 192 evaluation blocks. Cumulative attempt plus recovery doubles every count. The result supports replay as a forgetting-control mechanism, not stable forward transfer or zero-shot family recognition.

# 2026-10-27 — first-order family-level meta-adaptation is not supported

Two training-only leave-one-family-out diagnostics completed on opened controlled-observation data. The first trained a 624-parameter shared-feature initialization from scratch on three source families, excluding the target family completely. One target-family shared-weight step improved all eight evaluation laws in every fold/seed, but only by `0.08--1.25%`; the learned initialization was far worse than existing offline/replay features and usually worse than scratch. Comparisons with offline/replay were additionally target-exposed in the linear/Duffing/Van der Pol folds, so those ratios are not causal evidence.

The matched follow-up removed that asymmetry. Each fold/seed received 300 identical source-only pretraining updates, then branched into either 300 ordinary source updates or 300 first-order meta updates at equal task exposure. Both final branches received the same one-step update on eight target-family adaptation laws and were scored on eight disjoint laws. Meta/ordinary adapted error ratios were `1.117/0.846` for linear, `1.090/0.968` for Duffing, `1.003/1.002` for Van der Pol and `1.011/1.075` for pendulum. Only two of eight cells favored meta, and neither met the `0.80` aggregate gate. The target-family step improved every law but only `0.02--1.61%`; meta-versus-ordinary differences were already present before adaptation.

The matched result used eight common pretraining runs, eight ordinary and eight meta continuations, 7,200 outer optimizer updates, 2,400 meta inner steps, 57,600 training ridge solves and 256 evaluation blocks. Result SHA-256: `3dcc3bab540004678427becf0d09e1ecb4fc1f75dd6eea8b9a88a896fb17956f`. This closes the fixed one-step/first-order/learning-rate objective, not meta-learning generally.

# 2026-10-27 — passive32 bridge does not preserve controlled-probe accuracy

The frozen cross-mechanism feature map was evaluated with exactly 32 observations from two passive 16-step trajectories per opened pendulum development law, instead of 32 independent controlled probes. No training or optimizer work occurred. Both seeds retained strong correct-versus-cyclic law identity (`16/16` wins; cyclic/correct aggregate ratios `2.20` and `3.13`), but absolute prediction failed badly: passive error was `70.90x` and `50.21x` the matched controlled32 error, and was also worse than both random-feature and affine passive controls on every law. Classification: `NOT_SUPPORTED`.

This is evidence that active excitation/support is essential to the confirmed frozen representation/head under this schedule. It is not evidence that passive trajectories lack law information—the cyclic control shows the opposite—and it does not test a representation trained explicitly for passive-context inference. Exact work was 512 new passive steps, 1,024 repeated query steps, 6,144 RK4 RHS evaluations, 80 ridge factorizations, zero fits and zero optimizer updates. Result SHA-256: `9a0ea6c8631fd72d48ce1d4a48fb70ebeb79326852f0939b7973d83db638ea48`.

# 2026-10-27 — passive-episode-matched training partly repairs the bridge

Training the same compact feature/head mechanism on four passive episodes per training law reduced opened pendulum passive32 error to `0.149x` and `0.350x` the frozen controlled-trained model, winning all `16/16` laws in both seeds. Whole-law identity strengthened: cyclic wrong-law contexts were `9.75x` and `7.65x` worse, with `16/16` and `15/16` correct-context wins. This establishes that training/context-distribution mismatch was a material cause of the frozen bridge failure.

The full gate still failed. The two seeds achieved learned/random ratios `0.867` and `1.183`, with only `9/16` and `5/16` wins, and learned/affine ratios `1.130` and `1.536`, with `0/16` wins. The passive-trained representation therefore identifies laws better than it predicts off-trajectory responses and does not reliably exceed simple passive controls. Exact work: 14,848 one-step simulations/59,392 RHS evaluations, two fits/1,200 updates, 9,600 training ridge factorizations and 32 evaluation factorizations. Result SHA-256: `d9a985ad0d92f5a15eca0bd7f3e18e3d5ec4a04c1a894d46f0a65522788d8c4b`.

# 2026-10-27 — broader mechanism experience helps, but learned transfer features remain unreliable

With training volume and compute matched, a six-mechanism corpus produced much better learned features than three-family coefficient expansion on excluded quadratic-drag and deadzone laws, and it beat random and affine controls in every excluded case. It did not beat the supplied generic polynomial/trigonometric/tanh library. This is positive evidence that retained experience across mechanisms matters, within a shared oscillator interface; it is not evidence of arbitrary-mechanism learning.

Adding a learned residual to that generic library improved every excluded law, but the preregistered replicated gate failed because gain magnitude varied strongly by seed. Exact result: quadratic-drag learned/generic ratios `0.880` and `0.851`; deadzone `0.986` and `0.554`; all four cells retained `16/16` correct-versus-cyclic wins. Result SHA-256: `418a7564b6452073bca3491e43d954fc3ada25a7f0831d7b27833529f3028681`.

A zero-gradient rank-32 function-space SVD diagnostic separated optimization from representation. Mechanism-diverse SVD modes beat coefficient-only modes, but lost to random row-span subspaces on both excluded families. They were excellent on deadzone versus generic16 (`0.275x`) and catastrophically worse on quadratic drag (`33.05x`), while preserving `16/16` identity wins. Thus the current failure is not explained by Adam alone: unsupervised variance reconstruction is itself a poor transfer objective. Exact work was 35,328 repeated one-step simulations, 141,312 RHS evaluations, two SVDs, four QR factorizations and 320 compact ridge solves, with no fits or updates. Result SHA-256: `d79dea85d89460c8c3b1e033199c282bde1ea99fc19f211fdcb391a2e96bdc53`.

# 2026-10-27 — whole-family feature transport is narrow, not general

Six leave-one-mechanism-out folds trained residual features on five families and evaluated the sixth, with no held-family influence on learned features or normalization. Generic16+learned16 was capacity-matched against generic16+random16; generic16 alone remained the explicit supplied-structure baseline. Both seeds failed the predeclared aggregate test: learned/random was `1.014` and `1.012`, while learned/generic was `1.016` and `1.015`.

The result is structured rather than uniformly negative. Linear transport improved by `13.5--26.6%` versus random and `26.3--33.7%` versus generic; pendulum improved by `27.9--43.5%` versus both. Duffing, saturating and Rayleigh did not show repeatable improvement. Correct context beat cyclic wrong context on every one of the `144/144` held laws in both seeds, with aggregate ratios near `1853`, so the analytic head retains highly specific law information. The missing capability is repeatable prediction of a functional component absent from the source mechanisms, not generic context blindness. Result SHA-256: `968fd504777a07acce18ffc80fb3d076b9ae789ba068a8ca69eee36be8acb0b1`.

# 2026-10-27 — bounded compositional memory retains but does not learn robustly

Two fixed chronologies added two vector-valued residual modes after four acquisition systems and evaluated 12 later systems. Blind growth preserved earlier performance within `5.2--7.2%` in both orders. Deadzone modes improved later error by `5.7--6.7%`, beating shuffled and random additions on all `12/12` laws, but missed the frozen 20% effect gate. Quadratic-drag modes worsened all `12/12` future laws. Thus small dictionary growth can retain knowledge and sometimes add useful signal, but top residual modes are not reliably reusable elements.

The original run's only failure was an after-persistence score-counter defect. The saved 408 predictions and dictionaries were not repeated. Recovery regenerated 3,072 deterministic targets and rescored once; cumulative work is recorded separately. Recovered result SHA-256: `7c546b20aab96e7088b5d9917a25a1641497d99bdece022ff9fa7900a67eb9ae`.

An encounter-only admission rule then used four leave-one-system-out checks before future labels. It rejected quadratic (`1.0499x`, `0/4` wins) and deadzone (`0.9900x`, `3/4`, below the predeclared `0.80` ratio) in both orders. Future and retained predictions were consequently unchanged exactly. This demonstrates safe rejection but no accumulated forward benefit. Result SHA-256: `29b41c28c48a5557edcf6318de17f503fd19ff821779db82cca8ce2916eac434`.

# 2026-10-27 — callable RKHS memory gives strong forward transfer but interferes

Callable vector-valued RKHS elements were learned on 32 element-training states, admitted on a disjoint 32-state acquisition-validation bank and evaluated on a third 64-state future bank. Kernel bandwidth came only from center geometry; regularization and all gates were frozen. Three proposals were admitted: quadratic first (`0.578` acquisition ratio), deadzone first (`0.319`) and quadratic after deadzone (`0.534`). Each improved all `12/12` future laws, with future/no-update ratios `0.429`, `0.324` and `0.433`, and each beat shuffled, random and replay-mean callable controls on all laws.

The full result remains `NOT_SUPPORTED`. Order-0 deadzone was rejected at `0.8062`, just outside but not rounded into the `0.80` gate. More importantly, after deadzone then quadratic, prior deadzone error increased to `1.363x` and no law stayed within the 10% retention tolerance. Thus callable elements contain transferable behavior, but dense compact ridge inference suffers cross-element interference. Retained memory was 520 bytes for centers/bandwidth plus 1,024 bytes per accepted element; per-system inference was 34 or 36 coefficients. Exact work: 5,120 one-step simulations/20,480 RHS calls, 24 SVDs, 128 kernel-weight solves, 5,120 scalar kernel evaluations and 392 compact coefficient solves.

The frozen follow-up `context_only_rkhs_prefix_v1` is `NOT_SUPPORTED`. Context-only four-fold selection chose the full prefix in 46/48 cases. It preserved later quadratic transfer in the deadzone→quadratic order (`0.4220x` generic error) but failed prior deadzone retention (`1.3442x` historical stage-1 error; `1/12` within 10%). In the reverse order retention was exact, but later deadzone improvement was only `0.8856x`, missing the frozen `0.8` gate. This closes this exact selector on opened development without changing the rejected `0.8062` proposal or any threshold.

The distinct span-preserving coordinate test is also `NOT_SUPPORTED`. Residualizing each callable block against the preceding dictionary preserved the finite function span to machine precision but destroyed forward gains (`1.1015x`/`1.0268x` generic) and worsened retention (`2.4424x`/`3.6026x` historical stage 1). This demonstrates strong fixed-ridge coordinate sensitivity, not a successful consolidation rule or proof that ridge geometry is the sole cause.

Full-covariance callable-block empirical Bayes and a nonparametric acquisition-exemplar mixture are both `NOT_SUPPORTED`. The Gaussian prior's mean—not covariance—drives its large gains and failures. The exemplar mixture avoids mean averaging and produces strong order-0 forward transfer, but context weights do not separate components reliably in order 1 and neither order preserves stage-one performance. Both cumulative methods beat current-only memory on all prior-family laws, while often harming current-family prediction. Retained information is therefore present, but these context-only priors do not resolve stability versus plasticity.
# 2026-10-27 — function distillation retains encountered mechanisms, not unseen Rayleigh

The matched replay-versus-distillation consolidation test passed every frozen development gate in both seeds. Storing prior checkpoint response functions, rather than prior true-response examples, preserved earlier mechanisms (`0.0232/0.0582` of naive error; `16/16` wins), transferred to each mechanism when it entered the chronology (`8/8` wins in all four seed/family cells), and stayed within the declared 1.2× bound of true-response replay (`1.193/1.115`). It used the same `73,728` retained target bytes per seed as replay and no old-system memory at deployed inference.

That success does not extend to zero-update transfer on the already opened Rayleigh mechanism. Frozen distillation and replay checkpoints were virtually tied, while both lost to seed-matched random features. Classification for the Rayleigh follow-up is `NOT_SUPPORTED`. The evidence therefore favors distillation as an efficient rehearsal substitute for encountered controlled mechanisms, not as proof of reusable features for absent mechanisms or arbitrary dynamics.

## 2026-10-28 — online diagonal-Laplace continual update

A distinct weight-space sequential-posterior diagnostic completed under its
prospective budget.  It retained a 624-value diagonal empirical Fisher per seed
and compared precision-preconditioned target updates to an isotropic matched
control.  The diagonal arm preserved aggregate source error but retained only
`18/24` and `17/24` source laws within 10%, adapted `1.905x/1.587x` worse than
the saved unconstrained learner and `1.443x/1.238x` worse than isotropic.  It is
`NOT_SUPPORTED`.  The result limits this scale-dependent diagonal curvature
approximation; it is not a calibrated posterior and does not rule out richer
task-generative Bayesian models.  Exact work was 182 gradient evaluations, 662
ridge solves, 32 target updates, 30,720 evaluation rows, 10,752 repeated
transitions and 43,008 RK4 RHS evaluations.  Evidence:
`outputs/sho/autonomous_generic_sho_20261014/diagnostics/sequence_aware_online_laplace_v1/`.

## 2026-10-28 — sequential compact-head hyperposterior

A frozen-feature task-generative diagnostic updated a 32-dimensional Gaussian
population distribution from each encountered system's observation-conditioned
head posterior.  Its exact online moments matched batch recomputation below
`9e-15`.  It retained all `24/24` earlier laws but improved only marginally over
the frozen population prior (`0.995x/0.957x`) and was substantially worse than
the isotropic reset (`1.364x/1.307x`, `0/8` law wins in both seeds).  The result
is `NOT_SUPPORTED`.  It rejects useful accumulation in this learned-head
coordinate under the declared Gaussian approximation; it is not evidence
against all task-generative models.  Evidence:
`outputs/sho/autonomous_generic_sho_20261014/diagnostics/sequential_head_hyperposterior_v1/`.
# 2026-10-27 — controlled Rayleigh is identifiable under a supplied analytic vector-field span

The later-mechanism failure is not explained by missing information in the controlled 32 observations. A 16-coefficient generic analytic vector field inferred separately for each law achieved `1.44e-8` macro MSE and perfect `8/8` correct-versus-cyclic identity. Direct generic16 increment regression was better still at `8.35e-9`. Both are roughly three orders of magnitude better than the learned/random representations.

A matched 16-coefficient universal Gaussian-grid vector field retained identity and beat random features but was `170.5x` worse than the analytic vector field. Therefore the strong result depends materially on the supplied callable function span; it is not learned discovery or accumulated knowledge. The next problem is learning reusable vector-field atoms under the structural integration objective while holding out entire mechanisms, not another replay, prior, ridge, basis-resolution or neural-width sweep.
# 2026-10-27 — opaque learned acceleration atoms do not transport across mechanisms

Moving the same-capacity learner into continuous-time acceleration space did not solve whole-family transfer. It learned meaningful identity-sensitive structure and beat random atoms, but lost decisively to both a fixed universal RBF basis and the earlier direct increment models. Exact context-transition refinement recovered only about 18% and did not change the conclusion.

The combined evidence now separates three levels: controlled observations contain useful law information; generic smooth callable bases exploit some of it; and the supplied analytic library exploits far more. A 624-parameter opaque atom network trained for integration consistency did not discover a reusable span between those controls. This is a failure of this atom-learning objective/architecture, not proof that callable dictionary learning is impossible.
# 2026-10-27 — learned polynomial search is source-prior dominated

The sealed cached-data guidance comparison completed validly and is `NOT_SUPPORTED`. Learned guidance beat random candidate ordering by roughly 200x in aggregate and preserved `24/24` correct-versus-cyclic wins, but the accumulated guide, frozen guide, equal-compute linear-only guide and unconditional source-frequency prior produced the exact same candidate and predictions on every held VDP law. No selected model changed after accumulating Duffing experience.

This is a clean negative for the claimed accumulation mechanism and a positive for a much narrower fact: a supplied cubic grammar plus source-derived global support prior prevents catastrophic random-search misses. The next search test, if pursued, must balance support frequencies and hold out whole support structures so context-conditioned guidance cannot be replaced by an unconditional prior.
# 2026-10-27 — learned search needs compositional interaction structure

Balanced synthetic structures removed the source-frequency confound. The 171-parameter independent-term guide learned useful reusable search bias: about 70% lower aggregate error than random ordering, about 81% lower than an unconditional prior, `41–43/64` true held-support recoveries and perfect identity controls. Its accumulation gate still failed because most systems tied the equal-compute early guide; gains were concentrated in tail-risk reduction.

A whole-support categorical guide then failed decisively on unseen combinations (`0/64` support recovery; `2.46–2.52x` independent-guide error). The exact autoregressive alternative was computationally capped before results. The useful conclusion is narrower than “search works”: prior experience can guide candidate search inside a supplied grammar, but the policy must combine familiar terms into unseen structures while modeling interactions efficiently. No confirmation structure was opened.
# 2026-10-27 — pair interactions do not improve unseen-support guidance

At identical observations, search budget and coefficient adaptation, a 207-parameter unary-plus-pairwise energy guide was slightly worse than the 171-parameter independent-term guide (`1.039/1.071x` aggregate error; `5/64` wins) and recovered fewer true structures (`38/37` versus `43/41`). Accumulation again reduced rare tail failures but not a majority of systems. Static pair interactions therefore do not supply the missing compositional generalization on this controlled corpus.

### 2026-10-28 retained sequential adaptation

Sequence-aware meta-training produced the clearest positive adaptation signal
in this branch: after eight context-only pendulum encounters, both seeds reduced
error on eight disjoint pendulum laws by about 61–63%, outperforming matched
current-only training on every law. It did not pass the combined gate because
all eight represented Duffing laws forgot on disjoint query states. Average
A-GEM detected no conflict. Full per-system GEM did detect and project conflicts
and improved aggregate retention, but Duffing function retention still failed.
The bounded conclusion is that persistent shared-weight learning can reduce
later-system error in this supplied oscillator interface, while protecting only
the observed rows is inadequate for retaining off-row behavior. No fresh
confirmation or arbitrary-mechanism claim is made. See
`diagnostics/sequence_aware_plasticity_v1/`,
`diagnostics/sequence_aware_agem_v2_recovery/`, and
`diagnostics/sequence_aware_full_gem_v1/`.

The follow-up function-space constraint retained 24 source-training functions
on query64, rather than only their observation rows. It improved aggregate
source retention but did not retain held source systems and reduced the strong
pendulum adaptation benefit. The next scientific choice is therefore between
learning a distributional generator/replay model for source functions and
isolating mechanisms in separately retained parameter modules. Further
threshold, projection or exemplar-bank tuning is not supported by these data.

Distributional replay then sampled old functions from a full empirical
Gaussian fitted without family labels to source context+query functions. It
retained every held source law in both seeds, unlike fixed exemplars, and the
joint generator modestly beat a context/query-pairing-broken generator.
However, the broken generator also retained every law and hard projection
substantially weakened new-family adaptation. The conjunction is therefore
`NOT_SUPPORTED`: broader synthetic function coverage is a useful retention
signal, but this experiment does not establish that the learned joint
distribution is the cause or solve stability versus plasticity.

The subsequent soft function-space KL versus gradient-matched mean-only test
also failed the conjunction. Covariance-aware KL approximately halved final
pendulum error relative to unconstrained sequence learning, but worsened held
source retention; mean-only regularization retained source behavior better in
both seeds. Therefore the empirical covariance is not supported as retained
uncertainty under this update—it behaves primarily as an adaptation direction.
No covariance floor, subspace, coefficient or regularizer sweep is justified
from these opened outcomes.

## Independent PDEBench corpus

The first independently sourced corpus test used official PDEBench advection,
Burgers and reaction-diffusion field trajectories rather than another curated
oscillator basis.  A standard million-parameter Transformer received four full
field states as context and queried four later states by `(x,t)`.  It did not
demonstrate accumulated new-mechanism knowledge.  Replay helped one seed and
hurt the other; no-replay adaptation was better in both.  Context shuffling
left aggregate error unchanged, showing that this model mostly learned a
population space/time predictor instead of using the observed trajectory.

This is a substantive negative because it exposes a contract/shortcut problem
on independent data.  It is not evidence that PDE trajectories are
uninformative, nor that attention models are exhausted.  The next causal test
should expose the current field directly at each query and predict its next
increment, with a matched current-field shuffle.  That is a declared Markov
state interface correction, not another hidden analytic basis or a claim of
general PDE discovery.

The explicit Markov query did restore observation use: current-field shuffling
raised error `1.63–2.24x` and history shuffling `1.08–1.95x`.  Adaptation beat
the frozen source model in both seeds, but the conjunction failed because only
one seed beat scratch and retained source blocks reliably.  An exactly paired
follow-up then showed that real-example replay was worse than no replay on
target error in both seeds (`1.88x/1.66x`) while preserving aggregate source
performance.  The defensible capability is therefore: explicit current fields
and history support new-mechanism adaptation on this independent scalar-PDE
subset, but full-weight replay does not yet turn that adaptation into reliable
accumulated knowledge.
